- **The deletion decision, stated deliberately as the brief asked.** The `gd-*` renderer did **not** go, because guidance was never its only caller: `gdRenderDoc` draws the public study guides (since v01.74w), the report view builds on `gdTable`/`gdTimeline`/`gdBars`/`gdProsCons`, and the sign-in log uses `gdTable` and `gdCloseBtn`. Deleting the engine would have been deleting three live surfaces to tidy up after a fourth. What went is what only guidance called. Dead CSS was found by diffing class usage inside `<style>` blocks against the rest of the file — five blocks, all of which were live at `HEAD` and became dead in this commit, so none of them is a pre-existing fence being torn down on the side
- **The merge rule, and why "existing Classroom tick always stands" is right in both directions.** The brief requires that a newer Classroom tick is not overwritten by an older Profiler one. The alternative considered was max-date-wins; it is wrong, because if Profiler's date is the **newer** of the two, Classroom's earlier date is the true first completion and moving it forward would fabricate history and suppress every delta in between. Never overwriting satisfies the brief, is correct in the other direction too, matches the "an existing value stands" rule both `gdProgressWrite_` and `clProgressWrite_` already state, and makes the import **idempotent by construction** — a second run finds every tick present and writes nothing
- **The checker caught what the code change implied.** `check-classroom-content.py` asserts `tickable == sorted(readable lessons)` for every tier. Admitting modules to `clProgressValid_` breaks that assertion by design, so the checker moved with the code in the same commit — and the new assertions are stronger than the old ones, not merely accommodating

The brief predicted rotation would be due, on the reasonable assumption that this push would land on a later day; it did not. The clone was unshallowed anyway (55 → 1,145 commits) as the brief instructed, so SHA enrichment is available the moment it does fire. Oldest whole date group is still **thirteen sections dated 2026-09-04**
- **Two render traps, both worth the next session's time.** `HTML_CONFIG.STORAGE_TYPE` on Classroom is `sessionStorage`, and the page's single-tab enforcement **clears it on load** — so a session seeded from `add_init_script` is wiped before `clAdmitted()` reads it. And `_gasPost` is a top-level `function` declaration, so an init-script assignment to `window._gasPost` is **overwritten** by it. Both stubs must be installed *after* the page's own scripts have run. Recorded in the rules file's step 7
- **`gis_load_failed` is a harness artifact, not a regression.** The Profiler render aborts `accounts.google.com`, so Google's sign-in script fails to load. Confirmed identical on a pristine `HEAD` Profiler across three runs each
- **Both pre-existing failures re-confirmed against a pristine `HEAD` worktree**: `check-classroom-content.py` exits 1 on the same **24 errors** across ten stale `segment-*` lessons (a byte-identical error set — S2's work per curriculum plan §10.9), and `verify-profiler-roles.py` reports the same **2** progress-persistence failures with the same 13 × 4 matrix. Neither is caused nor fixed here

## [v05.46r] — 2026-09-13 01:24:48 AM EST

> See attached screenshots. I set the same GUIDANCE_PEER_TOKEN on both Profiler and Classroom's App Scripts' Script Properties and confirmed they are the same and have no trailing space. However, I cannot see the chip you are mentioning. What went wrong? Fix it.

### Fixed

**The v05.45r peer route compared its shared secret byte-for-byte and reported every upstream fault as a transport fault.** Both are defects introduced by that commit, and together they made a single silent symptom — no guidance chips on a dossier — cover four unrelated causes with no way to tell them apart. The developer set the token correctly on both sides and still saw nothing, which is exactly the failure this pair produces.

What was ruled out first, by evidence rather than assumption: Profiler is deployed at `v01.37g` and Classroom's route answers `denied` to a deliberately wrong token, so both sides were live; `profiler-companies.json` returns HTTP 200 and 152 KB on public Pages, and both projects' `EMBED_PAGE_URL` derive the identical base, so the scan's input was reachable. That left the comparison and the error reporting.

#### `googleAppsScripts/Classroom/Classroom.gs` — v01.22g

- `clHandleGuidancePeer_` now **trims both the stored property and the supplied token** before comparing. A value pasted out of an Apps Script execution log or a text editor routinely carries a trailing newline or space; it is invisible in the Script Properties UI, fatal under `!==`, and — because the refusal is deliberately the same flat `denied` a wrong guess gets — indistinguishable from having typed the secret wrong. Surrounding whitespace is never meaningful in a shared secret. **The boundary is unchanged**: a whitespace-only property trims to empty and still fails the 16-character floor, so trimming cannot open the route
- `guidanceMentions_()` is now called **inside a try/catch**. It fetches the company registry from public Pages, so it can throw; an escaping throw made Apps Script serve its own HTML error page at HTTP 200, which the caller then failed to parse and reported as a dead network. It now returns `{success:false, error:'mentions_failed', detail}` — JSON the proxy can relay

#### `googleAppsScripts/Profiler/Profiler.gs` — v01.38g

- `guidanceMentionsProxy_` **trims its own property** for the same reason, so the token that goes on the wire is clean regardless of how it was pasted
- **A parse failure is no longer reported as a transport failure.** The single `upstream_unreachable` catch spanned both the fetch and the `JSON.parse`, so an upstream exception, an Apps Script error page and a genuinely dead socket all produced the same word. The fetch and the parse now have separate handlers: a non-JSON body returns `upstream_not_json` carrying a 160-character snippet of what actually came back, while `upstream_unreachable` now means only what it says

### Changed

- **`README.md`** — Classroom `v01.22g`, Profiler `v01.38g`; `Last updated:` and `Repo version:`
- **`live-site-pages/gs-versions/Classroomgs.version.txt`**, **`live-site-pages/gs-versions/Profilergs.version.txt`** — bumped
- **`live-site-pages/gs-changelogs/Classroomgs.changelog.md`** (`Sections: 22/50`), **`live-site-pages/gs-changelogs/Profilergs.changelog.md`** (`Sections: 38/50`)

### Verified

- `node --check` on `.js` copies of both `.gs` files; `scripts/check-gas-inner-scripts.js` — 9 files, 86 inner blocks; `scripts/check-guidance-parity.py` still clean (the twelve watched functions are untouched)
- **The whitespace cases now pass in Node, all four of them**: a trailing newline on the property, a trailing space on the property, a trailing space from the caller, and different whitespace on both sides at once — every one previously `denied`, every one now returning the full 29-company answer
- **The boundary was re-proved, not assumed**: property unset, wrong token, whitespace-only property (trims to empty), sub-16-character property all still return a flat `denied`; an unknown `gpop` still returns `unknown_gpop`
- **An upstream throw no longer escapes** — with the registry made unreachable, the handler returns `mentions_failed` with the reason instead of letting the exception reach `doGet`
- **Each Profiler-side failure mode now names itself distinctly**: an HTML error page → `upstream_not_json` with a snippet, Classroom's own `mentions_failed` relayed intact, `denied` relayed intact, HTTP 403 → `upstream_http_403`, a thrown fetch → `upstream_unreachable`. Previously the first, second and fifth were all `upstream_unreachable`
- **The role gate is unchanged**: viewer and analyst → `ROLE_DENIED` with zero upstream calls; contributor and admin → relayed in exactly one call. `verify-profiler-roles.py` not required — no access-matrix value changed
- `scripts/check-readme-tree.py` 0 findings; `scripts/check-classroom-curriculum.py --strict` clean; `scripts/check-classroom-pipeline.py --selftest` 13 fixtures, 0 failures
- Neither page was touched, so no page version moved and `Profiler.html` remains byte-identical to v01.87w

### Added

**C3 session 2 — the guidance homecoming's federation slice.** Checklist items 2 and 3 of the C3 slice plan: the Admin lens re-hosts where the modules now live, and the mentions scan moves with the content it scans, behind one narrow token-gated route that Profiler proxies. Nothing moved between sessions 2 and 3, and Profiler's copy of the nine modules is untouched — session 3 still deletes it, together with `check-guidance-parity.py`.

#### `live-site-pages/Classroom.html` — v01.11w

- `clLoadAdminLens` / `clApplyAdminLens` — the `cl*` port of Profiler's `gdLoadAdminLens` / `gdApplyLens` on the light palette, with a small `clFetchJson` and a `.cl-lens` rule set keyed on `--cl-dis`. Gated by `clCan('reports')`: **admin only, and `CL_ROLE_CAPS` already said so**, which is why no access-matrix value changed. **No new server route** — `reports/reports-index.json` and `reports/<id>.report.json` are public Pages data under `CL_DATA_BASE`, the same bytes `ovFetchJson` reads in Profiler. Entries are filtered to `status === 'current'` carrying the module id in `overlayModules[]`, and each `guidanceOverlays[]` panel is inserted into `#cl-<sectionId>`
- Three behaviours differ from Profiler's version, each deliberately. **(a)** Guidance is read-only here, so a section has no `.cl-done-btn` to insert before; the lookup is kept for session 3 but a miss **appends to the section** rather than dropping the panel — the failure the brief named. **(b)** Company names inside panel prose are **not** linked to dossiers: `gdLinkCompanies` walks the registry rewriting text nodes and Classroom has no such helper, so adding one would be a page-wide feature rather than a lens port. **(c)** Term tooltips **are** bound on the panel, which Profiler's version does not do — the module render binds tips before the lens resolves, so a `{{term}}` in a future overlay would render unbound there. No overlay uses one today
- The lens is called at the end of `clRenderGuidanceDoc`, after the sections and the glossary exist. Every fetch failure is swallowed: the lens is an enhancement, never a blocker for the module

#### `googleAppsScripts/Classroom/Classroom.gs` — v01.21g

- `guidanceMentions_()` moved here **byte-identical** from `Profiler.gs`, verified by brace-matched extraction and diff. It scans module JSON, so after session 1 it can only answer where the content lives; its registry fetch resolves to the same public file because both pages sit at the site root, and its 6h script cache is per-script so no key collides with Profiler's
- `clHandleGuidancePeer_(e)` answers `?action=guidancepeer&t=…&gpop=mentions`, wired into `doGet` beside the existing action routes — the mirror of `scHandleCorpus_` in `Scraper.gs`. **Token-gated, never session-gated, and with no browser-facing door**: Profiler's `guidanceAllowed_` remains the single boundary deciding who may ask. A missing, wrong, empty or under-length token all return the same flat `denied`, so a probe cannot distinguish an unconfigured project from a wrong guess, and no default token ships in code
- Both functions sit **below `// CONTENT END`** with the rest of the guidance block, outside the C2 pipeline's write set by construction

### Changed

#### `googleAppsScripts/Profiler/Profiler.gs` — v01.37g

- `guidanceMentions_()` **removed** — moved, not copied. This is the first piece of Profiler's guidance slice actually to go; the twelve functions `check-guidance-parity.py` watches are untouched, and `guidanceMentions_` was never one of them
- `handleGuidanceOp_`'s `gop=mentions` branch is now `guidanceMentionsProxy_()`: a server-to-server `UrlFetchApp` call to `CLASSROOM_GUIDANCE_EXEC` on the **new** `GUIDANCE_PEER_TOKEN` Script Property, the same shape `handleNewsOp_` already uses for Scraper's corpus route and placed beside it. The token is deliberately **not** `CORPUS_TOKEN` — a different pair of peers, a different secret, rotated independently
- `guidanceAllowed_` is untouched and still runs first, and **`live-site-pages/Profiler.html` is not modified at all**, so `ovGuidanceMentionsLine` and `ovGuideApi('mentions')` are exactly as they were. That they still work unchanged was the test

Repo-only files:

- **`repository-information/PHASE6-CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md`** — the slice plan's **session 2 record**: both items landed, nothing moved between sessions 2 and 3, the three named behaviour differences above, the proofs, and the operator step this commit cannot perform. Prose notes only — neither Mermaid diagram depicts guidance, so **no pako regeneration was needed** (the same finding as session 1)

### Verified

- `node --check` on `.js` copies of both `.gs` files and on both of `Classroom.html`'s inline `<script>` blocks; `scripts/check-gas-inner-scripts.js` — 9 files, 86 inner blocks; `scripts/check-guidance-parity.py` still clean (9 modules + 3 helpers byte-identical, ids matched)
- **Playwright render + screenshot read of every new surface, served over HTTP** rather than `file://` so the lens's real `fetch` path runs against the real report files: **zero page errors** everywhere. Admin sees **twelve panels across five modules** (`nvidia-800vdc` 3, `power-infra-aidc` 3, `bess-bankability` 2, `china-policy-stack` 2, `utility-aidc-procurement` 2) and **zero** on the other four. The two doubly-anchored sections — `bess-bankability/counterparty` and `power-infra-aidc/markets`, each taking a panel from two different reports — carry **both**, so insertion appends and never replaces
- **The `reports` gate proved page-side by network, not just by paint**: a contributor opening an anchored module issues **zero** report-data requests and sees zero panels across all nine modules, while an admin issues exactly three (the index and the two current reports anchoring that module)
- **The stale-anchor path proved with a synthetic overlay** pointing at a deleted section id: the panel renders with its italic note *and* both content paragraphs, placed immediately before `#cl-glossary` in the same parent — nothing dropped
- **The token boundary proved in Node, per case**: property unset (with and without a token supplied), token set but absent / wrong / empty, and a sub-16-character property all return a flat `denied`; an unknown `gpop` returns `unknown_gpop`; only the authorised call reaches the registry fetch at all
- **The Profiler side proved in Node, per tier**: viewer and analyst → `ROLE_DENIED` with **zero** upstream calls; contributor and admin → relayed in exactly one call to the expected URL; token unset → `not_configured` with zero upstream calls; upstream 403, unreachable and non-JSON each return `success:false` rather than throwing, which the page's existing failure path renders as nothing
- **Equivalence**: `guidanceMentions_()` run from `Classroom.gs` returns byte-identical output to the same function run from `Profiler.gs` at `HEAD` — the same 29 companies with the same module hits
- `scripts/check-readme-tree.py` 0 findings; `scripts/check-classroom-curriculum.py --strict` clean; `scripts/check-classroom-pipeline.py --selftest` 13 fixtures, 0 failures (`--base origin/main` reports P1/P2 **by design** — this diff is a human C3 session deliberately outside the unattended committer's write set, which is the fence working, not a finding)
- **Two pre-existing failures confirmed byte-identical against a pristine `HEAD` worktree, neither caused nor fixed here**: `check-classroom-content.py` 24 errors on the stale `segment-*` lessons (S2's work, curriculum plan §10.9) and `verify-profiler-roles.py` 2 progress-persistence failures — its full 13-surface × 4-tier matrix passes unchanged

### Operator action required

- **`GUIDANCE_PEER_TOKEN` must be set to the same random 16+ character value in *both* the Profiler and Classroom Apps Script projects' Script Properties.** Until it is, Profiler answers `not_configured`, Classroom refuses `denied`, and the dossier's `✦ Covered in guidance modules` chips render nothing — silently and by design, exactly as the Coverage panel behaves without `CORPUS_TOKEN`. This is the only part of checklist item 3 that cannot be done in the repo, and the token must never be committed to any file here

### Notes
- **Fence held.** No guidance module authored, no lesson, no landscape, no dossier; no registry, report or Routine edited; `Scraper.gs`, `Profiler.gs` and `Profiler.html` not modified at all
- **The nine modules are deliberately duplicated** in `Profiler.gs` and `Classroom.gs` through C3 sessions 1–2. Profiler stays the place guidance is *studied* until session 3; no guidance item enters the drill before then, which is decision 6 unchanged

### Added
- **`repository-information/industry-guidance/large-load-interconnection-2026-09-analysis.md`** — new analysis and study source for the ninth Industry Guidance module: *Large-Load Interconnection — The Federal Rulebook Above the Fence*. Research synthesis over **public primary sources only**, with a claims ledger (fifty-plus rows), a "what the record does NOT say" section (ten items) and a freshness gate. Sources: the Federal Register full text of **Order No. 2023** (Docket RM22-14-000, 88 FR 61014, effective 2023-11-06) and **Order No. 2023-A** (RM22-14-001, 89 FR 27006, effective 2024-05-16); the six Federal Register notices instituting the large-load **FPA section 206** proceedings (91 FR 37968–37975, 2026-06-24); FERC's orders in the **PJM co-location** docket (193 FERC ¶ 61,217, 2025-12-18, EL25-49-000 *et al.*), **SPP HILL/HILLGA** (194 FERC ¶ 61,031, ER26-247-000) and **SPP CHILLS** (195 FERC ¶ 61,196, ER26-1323); FERC's Unified Agenda entry for **RM26-4-000** (91 FR 53150); the Sunshine Act notice for the 2026-06-18 open meeting (91 FR 36126); and the Texas Legislature's record for **SB 6** (89R, effective 2025-06-20)
- **`googleAppsScripts/Profiler/Profiler.gs`** — `guidanceDocLargeLoadInterconnection_()`, module id `large-load-interconnection-2026-09`, lane **The AI Data-Center Wave**, registered in `guidanceDocs_()` immediately after `utility-aidc-procurement-2026-08` so the lane stays contiguous. The load-bearing finding: **"large load" and "load interconnection" each appear zero times in the 336 Federal Register pages of Order No. 2023**, and zero in 2023-A along with "co-location" and "data cent" — the federal rulebook standardised generator interconnection three times in twenty years and never addressed load, which is the space the state large-load tariff grew into

Terms are deliberately **federal and narrow** (`Order No. 2023`, `show cause order`, `section 206`, `co-located load`, `cluster study`, `withdrawal penalty`, `RM26-4`, `High Impact Large Load`, `Contract Demand`, `behind the meter generation`) because the existing `topic-utility-procurement` seed already carries *interconnection*, *large load*, *co-location* and the RTO names — repeating them would split one band in two rather than open a new one

>
> **Follow-up prompts (same interaction, after the first research run hit the Fable usage cap):** "Switch to Opus 5 xhigh to continue" · "Try again" · "Try again"

### Added

- **`repository-information/SOLID-STATE-TRANSFORMERS-PRIMER.pdf`** — a 38-page educational primer on solid-state transformers for AI data centers: 10 chapters (executive summary; why 800 V DC; inside an SST stage by stage; SST vs transformer-rectifier unit and NVIDIA's Option A/B/C staging; technical limitations; US AIDC infrastructure limitations; the players layer by layer; positioning matrix and outlook; glossary; sources), 14 figures, 9 tables, 133 numbered sources tagged FIRST-PARTY / SECONDARY / REPO. Built from the Profiler corpus (15 dossiers, the 2026-09-08 `aidc-power-conversion` competitive report, the `nvidia-800vdc-2026-08` Industry Guidance module and the grid-equipment analysis) plus independent research prioritising first-party documents (NVIDIA's October 2025 and August 2026 papers, ORNL's 2026 MV-SST review, Siemens' DC-protection paper, Heron/Sungrow/ABB/Delta product pages, NERC's July-2024 event review and 2026 Level 3 alert, ERCOT/FERC/LBNL/Grid Strategies/NFPA FPRF documents, SEC exhibits)
- **`repository-information/sst-primer-print.html`** — the primer's single HTML source (BloombergNEF-style skin derived from the AIDC report's canonical stylesheet; inline `<figure>` references, numbered `<sup>` citations)
- **`repository-information/sst-primer-figures/`** — 16 SVG figures: four hand-drawn schematics (three-stage ISOP architecture, one cell unrolled with AFE/DAB/MFT, legacy-vs-TRU-vs-SST conversion chains, US grid-to-rack voltage ladder) and twelve matplotlib charts (current vs bus voltage, CHB cell count by service voltage and SiC class, NVIDIA's kW/mm² table, chain-vs-chain losses, timeline, LBNL/Grid Strategies demand, ERCOT queue and transformer lead times, the NERC July-2024 event, positioning map, demand-signal growth metrics)
- **`scripts/build-sst-primer-figures.py`** — regenerates the twelve data charts (matplotlib, AIDC report's validated six-hue palette; every plotted number is sourced or explicitly computed in the function docstrings)
- **`scripts/build-sst-primer-pdf.mjs`** — single-edition renderer (Chromium over the DevTools Protocol, same mechanism as `build-aidc-report-pdf.mjs`; `--png` proof mode)

### Changed

- **`CHANGELOG-archive.md`** — the 2026-09-03 date group (13 sections, `v04.35r`–`v04.47r`) rotated out of the active file with commit-SHA enrichment on every header (clone deepened to 1,104 commits first; 13 of 13 resolved). Active file now at `Sections: 89/100`
- **README tree** — entries for the five additions above (figures directory expanded file-by-file); `Last updated:` and `Repo version:` refreshed

### Notes

- **Findings the primer pins down that the corpus previously carried only secondhand.** (1) NVIDIA's October 2025 white paper specifies **single-ended 800 V DC** (POS/RTN/PE) and explicitly rejects the OCP ±400 V bipolar bus for lack of three-pole DC breakers — the most-misreported point in the topic; the rack accepts either. (2) NVIDIA rates the **MV rectifier and the SST at the same 98.5%+**; the "up to 5% efficiency / 45% copper" figures are architecture-vs-legacy-AC claims from the October 2025 blog, not SST-vs-TRU claims. (3) The August 2026 execution paper (held in `industry-guidance/sources/`) names **three TRU families for the first Option C block, including Zhonhen's Panama Architecture**, and attaches the ~2029 date specifically to the next-generation 34.5 kV-direct SST — confirming the repo report's framing first-hand. (4) The **GE Vernova dossier's hyperscaler commitment to buy 1,000 SSTs from 2027** if spec is met is the tier's single most important commercial datapoint and is now surfaced in the players chapter. (5) The **US-specific gating item is the DC arc-flash model**: NFPA FPRF's Phase 1 is focused on 800 Vdc data centers, is sponsored by Siemens/Schneider/Eaton/ABB/Mersen and "would span around three years" — a ~2029 deliverable against a 2027 Kyber ship date
- **Research method.** Four background subagents on Opus 5 (a repo-mining pass over the Profiler dossiers, reports and guidance modules; SST technology; US infrastructure limits; vendor positioning) after the first four runs on Fable 5.1 terminated on the usage cap. The technology and infrastructure agents downloaded and text-extracted 25+ primary PDFs (NVIDIA, NERC, ERCOT, DOE/LBNL, Siemens, ORNL, TI, Kolar). Aggregator domains the agents judged AI-generated (`mgrid.org`, `enkiai.com`, `gridreadiness.com`, `hiitio.com`) are cited only twice, both flagged as secondary and corroborated by first-party material. Domains that refused automated retrieval (opencompute.org, nerc.com HTML, ferc.gov, se.com, eaton.com press) are named in the Sources chapter
- **Two rendering decisions worth recording.** (1) **Page margins must be declared in the stylesheet's `@page` rule, not passed to `Page.printToPDF`** — with `preferCSSPageSize:true`, passing both made Chromium draw the repeated `<thead>` of a page-spanning table on top of the running header (seen on the positioning matrix); moving the margins into CSS (as `aidc-market-report-print.html` already does) fixed it and is commented in both the HTML and the script. (2) **Long prose belongs in `<figcaption>`, not in SVG `<text>`** — the hand-drawn schematics originally carried legend sentences as SVG text; they do not wrap, overflow the 740 px viewBox, and (a false alarm worth recording) appeared to vanish in headless screenshots because `--window-size` includes ~80 px of browser chrome that crops the viewport — the PDF renderer was never affected. The prose moved to captions regardless, where it wraps
- **Developer-branding rule applied to generated SVGs** — `build-sst-primer-figures.py` appends `<!-- Developed by: LightAISolutions -->` after the root element of every chart it writes (valid XML; ignored by renderers), and the four hand-drawn SVGs and the HTML source carry the same last line. The PDF is binary and carries none, matching the existing AIDC PDFs
- **Not changed:** `REPO-ARCHITECTURE.md` and the diagrams — they do not enumerate `repository-information/` documents (the AIDC report PDFs are likewise absent), so a new document there is not a structural change the diagrams depict. No page or GAS file was touched; no page/GAS changelog entry or version bump applies

- **README tree** entry for `check-readme-tree.py` notes it is run post-merge by the workflow

### Notes

- **It is wired in as a post-merge auto-fix, not the pre-merge gate the previous session's recommendation implied — and the change of design is the substance of this push.** A blocking gate would strand a real page or GAS fix behind a wrong number in a README table. The repo's one pre-merge blocking check, `Validate GAS served inner <script> syntax`, earns that position because a broken inner script breaks the running app in a way that is invisible until it is expensive; cosmetic display drift is not that
- **The step must never fail, and the reason is concrete rather than stylistic.** `Delete branch` and `Sweep stale claude branches` are both gated on `success()`, so a non-zero exit here would leave the `claude/*` branch on the remote — and the next push in that session would collide with Pre-Push Checklist item #5's push-once enforcement. **A cosmetic check would have broken branch cleanup.** Structural findings are therefore raised as `::error::` annotations, which render red in the run summary without failing the step
- **Verified against that exact failure mode.** The step body was run under `bash -e` — the shell GitHub Actions uses, where an unguarded non-zero exit aborts the step — across three cases in a sandboxed copy: **(A)** clean tree → no fix, no error, **exit 0**; **(B)** a drifted GAS display → `--fix` corrected it, second pass clean, **exit 0**; **(C)** a version file with no tree entry → `--fix` could not resolve it, `::error::` emitted, and the step still **reached its end and exited 0**. Case C is the one that matters: it proves a structural finding cannot break branch deletion
- **The step deviates from its two siblings by running unconditionally.** The AHK and payload steps guard on whether a `.ahk` or payload `.html` changed in the merge, because regeneration is meaningless otherwise. This one must also sweep drift left by pushes that predate it — v05.34r found five such displays — so a changed-files guard would leave exactly the backlog the step exists to clear
- **No new job permissions or setup were needed.** `git config user.name/email` is set in the merge step and is repo-local, so it persists across steps in the same job; `python3` is pre-installed on `ubuntu-latest` and the script is stdlib-only
- **Confirmed the step cannot conflict with the AHK sync**: the README tree carries **zero** `ahk.changelog.md` version displays, so the post-merge AHK regeneration has nothing in the tree to disagree with
- **Verification:** `yaml.safe_load` parses the workflow; the step resolves to position **16 of 18** in the `auto-merge` job, between the payload sync and `Delete branch`, carrying the same `if: steps.guard.outputs.skip != 'true' && success()` as its siblings. `check-readme-tree.py` on the real tree returns **0 findings** across 10 page + 8 GAS displays
- **`Sections: 103/100`** — **98 non-exempt** after excluding the three sections dated today, so rotation is **not** due on this push despite the counter reading past capacity. It fires on the next push landing on a later EST day. The clone is **already unshallowed** (1,096 commits, deepened at v05.33r), so SHA enrichment will resolve when it does

## [v05.34r] — 2026-09-10 06:52:29 AM EST

> **Prompt:** "audit the README tree version displays"

### Added

- **`scripts/check-readme-tree.py`** — compares every `vXX.XXw` and `vXX.XXg` display in the README tree against `live-site-pages/html-versions/` and `gs-versions/`, reporting three problem classes: **drift** (tree disagrees with the source file), **missing** (a version file exists but no tree entry links to its changelog) and **orphan** (a tree entry links to a changelog with no version file behind it). `--json` for machine-readable output; `--fix` syncs drifted displays to their version file — never the reverse, and never `missing` or `orphan`, which are structural and need a human. Exit 0 clean, 1 on any finding

### Fixed

- **Five stale GAS version displays in the README tree**, each behind by exactly one bump: `MasterACL` v01.13g → **v01.14g**, `Receipts` v01.28g → **v01.29g**, `globalacl` v01.07g → **v01.08g**, `testauthgas1` v01.06g → **v01.07g**, `testauthhtml1` v01.06g → **v01.07g**

### Changed

- **[PC-README-TREE] #7 now specifies a verification step, not only an instruction** — run `python3 scripts/check-readme-tree.py` before committing any commit that bumped a page or GAS version. The rule records *why* the change was made, so a future reader does not undo it as redundant
- **The obligation is now restated at both triggers, [PC-GS-VERSION] #1 and [PC-HTML-VERSION] #2** — with the reason it is duplicated rather than left as a cross-reference
- **README tree** gains an entry for the new script

### Notes

- **The audit's finding is lopsided in a way that identifies the cause.** All **ten** page displays matched; **five of eight** GAS displays were stale. The page side was clean only because v05.33r had swept it by hand the day before, so the honest reading is not "HTML is fine and GAS is not" — it is that **the display sync is missed on both paths, and only a manual sweep had recently corrected one of them**
- **Every drift was exactly one bump (0.01), never more.** That rules out a systemic gap that had been accumulating for months, and points instead at a per-session miss: each of the five was the most recent `.gs` bump for its project, made by a session that updated the two files [PC-GS-VERSION] #1 names and stopped there
- **The structural cause is that the obligation and its trigger lived in different rules.** [PC-GS-VERSION] #1 and [PC-HTML-VERSION] #2 say what to bump; only [PC-README-TREE] #7 — a long rule about tree structure, link format and origin labels — says the tree display must move too. **A session bumping a `.gs` has no reason to open #7 at all.** That is why the fix restates the obligation at both triggers rather than only adding the checker
- **The instruction was not unclear, and that is the point.** #7's sentence was specific and named both trigger rules by ID. It was still missed five times out of eight. Per the Think Before Asserting gate's Trigger D — *"'I'll remember next time' is NOT a structural fix"* — a clear instruction that does not hold needs a mechanism, which is what the repo's other `scripts/check-*.py` checkers already are for their rules
- **The checker found itself.** Adding `scripts/check-readme-tree.py` is a new file, which [PC-README-TREE] #7 requires be added to the tree — so the script's own arrival was the first change its rule governed
- **Verification:** `check-readme-tree.py` reproduced the hand audit exactly (5 drift, 0 missing, 0 orphan), `--fix` corrected all five, and re-runs in both readable and `--json` modes return **0 findings, exit 0**. `git diff` confirms **exactly five changed lines in README.md**, all GAS displays, each +0.01, with nothing else touched
- **No page or GAS source file was modified**, so no page/GAS version bumps and no page changelog entries were due
- **`Sections: 100/100`** — **98 non-exempt** after excluding the **two** sections dated today (this one and v05.33r), so rotation is **not** due on this push despite the counter reading at capacity. It fires on the next push that lands on a later EST day, when both stop being exempt and the count reaches 100. **Deepen the clone before that push** — SHA enrichment needs history the shallow clone does not have

## [v05.33r] — 2026-09-10 03:31:19 AM EST

> **Prompt:** "promote the staleness check to the templates"

### Added

- **The cache-busting reload and the first-load staleness check are now TEMPLATE code in both `HtmlAndGasTemplateAutoUpdate-auth.html.txt` and `-noauth.html.txt`, and are propagated to all ten versioned pages.** `ovFreshUrl`, `ovReloadFresh` and `ovDocVersion` sit together in the template region, and the first-poll guard sits in the `if (!currentVersion)` branch. Every page created from either template inherits both from now on

### Changed

- **All ten versioned pages bumped** — Classroom v01.09w, MasterACL v01.06w, Profiler v01.86w, Receipts v01.37w, Scraper v01.72w, gas-project-creator v01.04w, globalacl v01.06w, testauthgas1 v01.04w, testauthhtml1 v01.04w, text-compare v01.02w — each with its `<meta build-version>` and page changelog entry
- **`Profiler.html`'s two `PROJECT OVERRIDE` blocks removed and the code realigned to the template text.** The page carried this logic as a deliberate deviation; now that it is template code the markers would make a future propagation stop for no reason. Both blocks were verified **byte-identical to the template** afterwards, and the page's override count fell 10 → 6
- **The "currently `Profiler.html` only" scoping removed from all three rules** — [PC-HTML-SOURCE] #3 and [PC-HTML-VERSION] #2 in `CLAUDE.md`, and the build-version bullet in `.claude/rules/html-pages.md`. The meta tag is now load-bearing corpus-wide, and #2 says so at the point where the bump is specified
- **`Profilerhtml.changelog.md` rotated** — the oldest whole date group (**seven sections dated 2026-08-22**) moved to `Profilerhtml.changelog-archive.md` with SHA enrichment on all seven headers

### Notes

- **The templates never had the cache-busting reload either, so this promoted two dependent fixes rather than one.** Both templates carried three bare `window.location.reload()` calls and zero `ovReloadFresh` — the v01.30w cache fix had been a Profiler-only override since the day it was written. The staleness check *calls* `ovReloadFresh`, so promoting it alone would not have compiled. **Three reload sites were converted per file across all eleven files** (maintenance-mode version change, ordinary version change, GAS version change)
- **Ordering mattered in the edit itself:** the reload-site replacement had to run **before** the helper block was inserted, because `ovReloadFresh`'s own fallback line contains `window.location.reload()` and a blanket replacement would have made it call itself
- **[PC-TEMPLATE-PROP] #19's override scan was run and cleared all nine pages.** Four carry `PROJECT OVERRIDE` markers — Receipts (9), testauthhtml1 (9), testauthgas1 (5), Scraper (4) — and **none sits in the reload or polling region**: they are CSP tags, a themed logo, OAuth scopes, a notification panel, test session durations, UI toggle lists and SSO-indicator removals. No propagation was blocked and no page needed the user's adjudication
- **Two pages did not match the standard shape and were handled rather than skipped.** `text-compare.html` has its whole polling block **minified onto single lines**, so the exact-string anchors used elsewhere matched nothing; the promoter anchors on `if (!currentVersion) {` and on the reload call text instead, which is shape-independent. `gas-project-creator.html` carries **three** `build-version` matches because **line 1551 is a generator that writes a meta tag into pages it creates** — that string was deliberately left at `v01.00w`, since bumping it would stamp every newly created page with this repo's version
- **`404.html` and `index.html` were excluded and this is correct** — neither has the polling block, a version file, or a `build-version` meta tag, so neither is a tracked page
- **Verified in Chromium across all ten pages, twenty page loads, zero page errors.** For each page: the helpers resolve as functions; `ovDocVersion()` returns that page's **newly bumped** version, which proves both the meta bump landed and the leading-`v` normalisation is right; a matching version file produces **0** reloads; a version file ahead of the document produces **exactly 1**
- **The cost of this promotion is nine more places where a forgotten meta bump costs a wasted reload.** The loop guard bounds that to one reload per visitor per version, and [PC-HTML-VERSION] #2 already required both bumps — but the obligation is now real on every page rather than one, which is why the rule was amended at the point of the bump rather than only in #3
- **`Sections: 99/100`** — 98 non-exempt after excluding the one section dated today, so no repo rotation was due. The **page** rotation that did fire was Profiler's, which crossed its cap of 50 when yesterday's sections stopped being exempt
